# Require axes by name

## Goal

Move `axis` and `axes` behind `...` wherever a bare number could be mistaken
for data:

```r
# Before
rray_sum(x, 2)

# After
rray_sum(x, axes = 2)
```

`rray_add(x, 2)` and `rray_sum(x, 2)` look the same today but mean very
different things. Naming the axis removes that doubt.

## The rule

Require a name when a bare value could be mistaken for something else.

- `rray_sum(x, 2)`: the `2` could be data, so `axes` must be named.

- `rray_axis_names(x, 2)`: the function name says "axis", so no name is needed.

- `rray_rep(x, ..., times, axes)`: two numbers side by side, so both are named.
  This is already the case.

- `rray_slice_axis(x, i, ..., axis)`: `i` and `axis` are both numbers, so
  `axis` is named even though "axis" is in the function name. This is already
  the case.

## What changes

| Function | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| `rray_sum()`, `rray_prod()`, `rray_mean()`, `rray_all()`, `rray_any()`, `rray_max()`, `rray_min()` | `(x, axes, ..., na_rm = FALSE)` | `(x, ..., axes, na_rm = FALSE)` |
| `rray_locate_max()`, `rray_locate_min()` | `(x, axis, ..., na_rm = FALSE)` | `(x, ..., axis, na_rm = FALSE)` |
| `rray_cumulative_all()`, `rray_cumulative_any()`, `rray_cumulative_max()`, `rray_cumulative_min()`, `rray_cumulative_prod()`, `rray_cumulative_sum()` | `(x, axis)` | `(x, ..., axis)` |
| `rray_reverse()` | `(x, axes)` | `(x, ..., axes)` |
| `rray_unstack()` | `(x, axis)` | `(x, ..., axis)` |

## What stays the same

- Functions with "axis" or "axes" in the name and only one number:
  `rray_axis_names()`, `rray_axis_title()`, `rray_set_axis_names()`,
  `rray_set_axis_title()`, `rray_insert_axes()`, `rray_remove_axes()`,
  `rray_permute_axes()`.

- Functions that already follow the pattern: `rray_move_axes()`, `rray_rep()`,
  `rray_rep_each()`, `rray_roll()`, `rray_roll_each()`, `rray_split()`,
  `rray_slice_axis()`, `rray_slice_assign_axis()`, `rray_combine()`,
  `rray_stack()`.

- Functions without an axis: setters like `rray_set_names()`, subscripts like
  `i` in `rray_extract()` and `rray_slice_rows()`, `dimensions` in
  `rray_broadcast()`, and every binary op like `rray_add(x, y)`.

- Old plans in `plans/`. They record past decisions and are not rewritten.

## Error

Use rlang's `check_dots_empty0()`, like `rray_rep()` and `rray_roll()` already
do. A positional axis then gives:

```
`...` must be empty.
✖ Problematic argument:
• ..1 = 2
ℹ Did you forget to name an argument?
```

No custom error.

## How to implement

Make one commit per function, in the order of the checklist below. Every
commit must pass `devtools::test()` and `devtools::check()` on its own, so a
commit changes the function and every caller of it at once.

For each function:

1. Move `axis` or `axes` behind `...` in the signature. Add
   `check_dots_empty0(...)` as the first line if the function doesn't already
   have it.

2. Document `...` with `@inheritParams rlang::args_dots_empty`, like
   `R/rep.R` and `R/roll.R` do. Put it above every `@param`, with a blank
   line on either side:

   ```r
   #' @inheritParams rlang::args_dots_empty
   #'
   #' @param x An array.
   #'
   #' @param axis A single integer representing the axis to unstack along.
   ```

   `R/reduce.R` and `R/locate.R` already have it in the right place, so
   reduce and locate functions skip this step.

3. Name the axis at every call site of the function:

   - Roxygen examples and prose in `R/`, including other functions' docs.
     For example, `R/cumulative.R` mentions `rray_sum(x, 1L)`.

   - Tests in `tests/testthat/`.

   - `README.Rmd` and `vignettes/`.

   - Test helpers that mirror the signature, such as
     `rray_sum_forced_fallback()` in `tests/testthat/helper-reduce-sum.R` and
     `rray_mean_forced_fallback()` in `tests/testthat/helper-reduce-mean.R`.
     Change their signature in the same commit as the function they mirror.

4. Add a test next to the function's other argument tests:

   ```r
   test_that("`axes` must be named", {
     expect_snapshot(rray_sum(1:3, 1), error = TRUE)
   })
   ```

   Use `axis` instead of `axes` in the test name where that is the argument.

5. Run `Rscript -e "devtools::document()"`, then
   `Rscript -e "devtools::build_readme()"` if `README.Rmd` changed.

6. Run `Rscript -e "testthat::snapshot_accept()"` only after reviewing the
   snapshot diff. Existing snapshots record the calls, so they change too.

7. Run `air format .` and `Rscript -e "devtools::test()"`.

8. Tick the function off in the checklist below in the same commit.

9. Commit with a one sentence message, such as
   ``Require `axes` to be named in `rray_sum()` ``.

All commits land in one pull request from `feature/dots`.

#' Reduce an array along axes
#'
#' @description
#' - `rray_sum()` computes the sum along the specified `axes`.
#'
#' - `rray_prod()` computes the product along the specified `axes`.
#'
#' - `rray_mean()` computes the mean along the specified `axes`.
#'
#' - `rray_all()` checks if all values are `TRUE` along the specified `axes`.
#'
#' - `rray_any()` checks if any value is `TRUE` along the specified `axes`.
#'
#' - `rray_max()` computes the maximum along the specified `axes`.
#'
#' - `rray_min()` computes the minimum along the specified `axes`.
#'
#' @details
#' The dimensionality of `x` is retained in the result, with the reduced axes
#' collapsed to size 1.
#'
#' @section Sum:
#' Logicals are summed as integers. If an integer sum doesn't fit in an
#' integer, an error is thrown. Only the final sum is checked, and `NA` wins
#' over an overflow:
#'
#' ```r
#' x <- c(.Machine$integer.max, 1L, -1L)
#' rray_sum(x, 1L) # .Machine$integer.max
#'
#' x <- c(.Machine$integer.max, 1L, NA)
#' rray_sum(x, 1L) # NA
#' ```
#'
#' @section Product:
#' Logicals and integers are cast to double.
#'
#' When `NA` and `NaN` are both present, you get one of them, but which one
#' depends on the platform and the order of the values. Either way, `is.na()`
#' is `TRUE`. Integers can hit this too, since a product that overflows to
#' `Inf` becomes `NaN` when multiplied by `0`:

#' @export
rray_any <- function(x, axes, ..., na_rm = FALSE) {
  check_dots_empty0(...)
  .Call(ffi_rray_any, x, axes, na_rm, environment())
}

#' @export
rray_max <- function(x, axes, ..., na_rm = FALSE) {

  .Call(ffi_rray_max, x, axes, na_rm, environment())
}

#' @export

  .Call(ffi_rray_min, x, axes, na_rm, environment())

#' Locate the maximum or minimum along an axis
#'
#' @description
#' - `rray_locate_max()` finds the position of the maximum along `axis`.
#'
#' - `rray_locate_min()` finds the position of the minimum along `axis`.
#'
#' @details
#' The dimensionality of `x` is retained, with `axis` collapsed to a dimension
#' of 1.
#'
#' Ties return the first position, like [which.max()] and [which.min()]:
#'
#' ```r
#' rray_locate_max(c(1, 3, 3), 1L) # 2
#' ```
#'
#' Missing values are infectious, so the result is `NA` if any value along
#' `axis` is `NA` or `NaN`. Use `na_rm = TRUE` to skip them:
#'
#' ```r
#' rray_locate_max(c(1, NaN, 3), 1L) # NA
#' rray_locate_max(c(1, NaN, 3), 1L, na_rm = TRUE) # 3
#' ```
#'
#' When there is no position to return, the result is `NA`. This happens when
#' `axis` has dimension 0, or when every value is missing and `na_rm = TRUE`:
#'
#' ```r
#' rray_locate_max(c(NA, NA), 1L, na_rm = TRUE) # NA
#' ```
#'
#' @inheritParams rlang::args_dots_empty
#'
#' @param x An array.
#'
#' @param axis A single integer giving the axis to locate along.
#'
#' @param na_rm If `TRUE`, missing values are skipped.
#'
#' @returns
#' An integer array with the same dimensionality as `x`, but with the dimension
#' along `axis` reduced to 1.
#'
#' @name rray-locate
#' @examples
#' x <- array(c(3L, 1L, 2L, 4L, 6L, 5L), c(3L, 2L))
#'
#' # Position of the maximum going down the rows
#' rray_locate_max(x, 1L)
#'
#' # Position of the minimum going across the columns
#' rray_locate_min(x, 2L)
NULL

#' @rdname rray-locate
#' @export
rray_locate_max <- function(x, axis, ..., na_rm = FALSE) {
  check_dots_empty0(...)
  .Call(ffi_rray_locate_max, x, axis, na_rm, environment())
}

#' @rdname rray-locate
#' @export
rray_locate_min <- function(x, axis, ..., na_rm = FALSE) {
  check_dots_empty0(...)
  .Call(ffi_rray_locate_min, x, axis, na_rm, environment())
}
#' Accumulate along an axis
#'
#' @description
#' - `rray_cumulative_all()` checks if all values are `TRUE` so far along
#'   `axis`.
#'
#' - `rray_cumulative_any()` checks if any value is `TRUE` so far along
#'   `axis`.
#'
#' - `rray_cumulative_max()` computes the maximum so far along `axis`.
#'
#' - `rray_cumulative_min()` computes the minimum so far along `axis`.
#'
#' - `rray_cumulative_prod()` computes the product so far along `axis`.
#'
#' - `rray_cumulative_sum()` computes the sum so far along `axis`.
#'
#' @details
#' Each position along `axis` summarizes every position up to and including
#' it.
#'
#' @section All / Any:
#' `x` must be logical. Missing values follow [all()] and [any()], so a later
#' `FALSE` or `TRUE` can settle an earlier `NA`:
#'
#' ```r
#' rray_cumulative_all(c(TRUE, NA, FALSE), 1L) # TRUE, NA, FALSE
#' rray_cumulative_any(c(FALSE, NA, TRUE), 1L) # FALSE, NA, TRUE
#' ```
#'
#' @section Min / Max:
#' `x` must be logical, integer, or double, and the result keeps the type of
#' `x`. Once a line hits `NA` or `NaN`, the rest of it stays missing. A later
#' `NA` replaces an earlier `NaN`, like [max()] and [min()]:
#'
#' ```r
#' rray_cumulative_max(c(1, NaN, 2, NA, 3), 1L) # 1, NaN, NaN, NA, NA
#' ```
#'
#' For logical `x`, a later `TRUE` or `FALSE` does not settle an earlier `NA`,
#' unlike `rray_cumulative_any()` and `rray_cumulative_all()`:
#'
#' ```r
#' rray_cumulative_max(c(NA, TRUE), 1L) # NA, NA
#' rray_cumulative_any(c(NA, TRUE), 1L) # NA, TRUE
#' ```
#'
#' @section Product:
#' `x` must be logical, integer, double, or complex. Logicals and integers are
#' cast to double.
#'
#' When `NA` and `NaN` are both present, you get one of them, but which one
#' depends on the platform and the order of the values. Either way, `is.na()`
#' is `TRUE` from that point on:
#'
#' ```r
#' rray_cumulative_prod(c(2, NA, NaN), 1L) # 2, NA, NA or NaN
#' ```
#'
#' @section Sum:
#' `x` must be logical, integer, double, or complex. Logicals are cast to
#' integer.
#'
#' An integer sum errors as soon as a running total overflows, even if a
#' later value would bring it back in range. This differs from [rray_sum()],
#' which only checks the final total:
#'
#' ```r
#' x <- c(.Machine$integer.max, 1L, -1L)
#' rray_sum(x, 1L) # .Machine$integer.max
#' rray_cumulative_sum(x, 1L) # Error
#' ```
#'
#' Once a line is `NA`, it stays `NA`, and a later overflow does not error.
#'
#' When `NA` and `NaN` are both present, you get one of them, but which one
#' depends on the platform and the order of the values. Either way, `is.na()`
#' is `TRUE` from that point on:
#'
#' ```r
#' rray_cumulative_sum(c(2, NA, NaN), 1L) # 2, NA, NA or NaN
#' ```
#'
#' @param x An array.
#'
#' @param axis A single integer. The axis to accumulate along. `1`
#'   accumulates down rows, `2` accumulates across columns, and so on.
#'
#' @returns
#' An array with the same dimensions and names as `x`.
#'
#' @name rray-cumulative
#' @examples
#' x <- array(c(TRUE, TRUE, FALSE, TRUE, NA, TRUE), c(2L, 3L))
#' x
#'
#' # Down rows
#' rray_cumulative_all(x, 1L)
#' rray_cumulative_any(x, 1L)
#'
#' # Across columns
#' rray_cumulative_all(x, 2L)
#' rray_cumulative_any(x, 2L)
#'
#' x <- array(c(3L, 1L, 2L, 5L, 4L, 0L), c(2L, 3L))
#' x
#'
#' # Running maximum and minimum across columns
#' rray_cumulative_max(x, 2L)
#' rray_cumulative_min(x, 2L)
#'
#' # Running product down rows

74 more lines cut

cat R/unstack.R R/rep.R | head -120; cat tests/testthat/helper-reduce-sum.R tests/testthat/helper-reduce-mean.R tests/testthat/helper-cumulative.R tests/testthat/helper-locate.R
#' Unstack an array
#'
#' @description
#' `rray_unstack()` splits an array along `axis` and then removes that `axis`
#' from each of the resulting arrays. The result is a list of arrays that have
#' a dimensionality 1 less than `x` itself.
#'
#' Using [rray_stack()] along the same `axis` reconstructs `x`.
#'
#' @details
#' Names along `axis` become the names of the returned list. Names on the
#' surviving axes are carried over to their new locations.
#'
#' Because the dimensionality is reduced by 1, `x` must have a dimensionality
#' of at least 2 to begin with.
#'
#' @param x An array.
#'
#' @param axis A single integer representing the axis to unstack along.
#'
#' @returns
#' A list of arrays with the same dimensions as `x`, except that `axis` has
#' been removed.
#'
#' @seealso [rray_stack()]
#'
#' @export
#' @examples
#' x <- array(1:6, c(2, 3))
#'
#' # One array per row
#' rray_unstack(x, 1)
#'
#' # One array per column
#' rray_unstack(x, 2)
#'
#' # Names along the axis become list names
#' y <- array(1:4, c(2, 2), dimnames = list(c("a", "b"), c("x", "y")))
#' rray_unstack(y, 2)
#'
#' # Unstacking and stacking along the same axis are inverses
#' rray_stack(!!!rray_unstack(x, 2), .axis = 2)
rray_unstack <- function(x, axis) {
  .Call(ffi_rray_unstack, x, axis, environment())
}
#' Repeat an array
#'
#' @description
#' - `rray_rep()` repeats whole axes of `x` in bulk, i.e. repeat all of the
#'   columns 5 `times`. It can repeat several `axes` at once, with one `times`
#'   per axis.
#'
#' - `rray_rep_each()` repeats each individual element of the `axis` of `x`
#'   separately, i.e. repeat the first column 2 `times`, the second column 3
#'   `times`, and so on. For convenience, you can also provide a single number
#'   to repeat each element along the `axis` the same number of times.
#'
#' These are the array versions of `vctrs::vec_rep()` and
#' `vctrs::vec_rep_each()`, which repeat along the size of a vector rather than
#' along an axis.
#'
#' @details
#' Names repeat alongside the data, so a repeated axis can come back with
#' duplicate names. Every other axis keeps its names untouched.
#'
#' @inheritParams rlang::args_dots_empty
#'
#' @param x An array.
#'
#' @param times For `rray_rep()`, a vector of integers greater than or equal
#'   to 0, either size 1 or the size of `axes`.
#'
#'   For `rray_rep_each()`, a vector of integers greater than or equal to
#'   0. It is recycled to the dimension of `x` along `axis`.
#'
#' @param axes An integer vector of axes to repeat along.
#'
#' @param axis A single integer representing the axis to repeat along.
#'
#' @returns
#' An array with the same dimensions as `x`, except along the repeated axes.
#'
#' @name rray-rep
#' @examples
#' x <- array(1:6, c(3, 2))
#'
#' # All rows in bulk, twice
#' rray_rep(x, times = 2, axes = 1)
#'
#' # Each row individually, twice
#' rray_rep_each(x, times = 2, axis = 1)
#'
#' # Columns rather than rows
#' rray_rep(x, times = 2, axes = 2)
#'
#' # Rows and columns, twice each
#' rray_rep(x, times = 2, axes = c(1, 2))
#'
#' # Rows twice and columns three times
#' rray_rep(x, times = c(2, 3), axes = c(1, 2))
#'
#' # A different number of repeats per row
#' rray_rep_each(x, times = c(1, 2, 3), axis = 1)
#'
#' # Zeroes drop slices entirely
#' rray_rep_each(x, times = c(1, 0, 2), axis = 1)
#'
#' # Names repeat with the data, duplicates and all
#' y <- array(1:6, c(3, 2), dimnames = list(c("a", "b", "c"), c("x", "y")))
#' rray_names(rray_rep(y, times = 2, axes = 1))
#' rray_names(rray_rep_each(y, times = 2, axis = 1))
NULL

#' @rdname rray-rep
#' @export
rray_rep <- function(x, ..., times, axes) {
  check_dots_empty0(...)
  .Call(ffi_rray_rep, x, times, axes, environment())
}

rray_sum_forced_fallback <- function(x, axes, ..., na_rm = FALSE) {
  check_dots_empty0(...)
  .Call(ffi_test_rray_sum_forced_fallback, x, axes, na_rm, environment())
}
rray_mean_forced_fallback <- function(x, axes, ..., na_rm = FALSE) {
  check_dots_empty0(...)
  .Call(ffi_test_rray_mean_forced_fallback, x, axes, na_rm, environment())
}
cumulative_reference <- function(x, axis, f) {
  dimensions <- dim(x)
  axes <- c(axis, setdiff(seq_along(dimensions), axis))

  out <- apply(x, axes[-1L], \(line) Reduce(f, line, accumulate = TRUE))
  out <- array(out, dimensions[axes])
  out <- aperm(out, order(axes))

  dimnames(out) <- dimnames(x)
  out
}
base_locate <- function(x, axis, fn, na_rm) {
  lane <- function(values) {
    if (!na_rm && anyNA(values)) {
      return(NA_integer_)
    }

    out <- fn(values)
    if (length(out)) out else NA_integer_
  }

  dimensions <- dim(x)
  out_dimensions <- dimensions
  out_dimensions[[axis]] <- 1L
  margins <- setdiff(seq_along(dimensions), axis)

  if (length(margins) == 0L) {
    return(array(lane(x), out_dimensions))
  }

  array(apply(x, margins, lane), out_dimensions)
}

cat .claude/hooks/deny-shell-edits.sh .claude/settings.json; grep -nE "rray_(sum|prod|mean|all|any|max|min|locate_max|locate_min|cumulative_[a-z]+|reverse|unstack)\(" bench/*.R R/extremum.R R/stack.R tests/testthat/test-extremum.R README.Rmd vignettes/toolkit.Rmd
#!/usr/bin/env bash

cmd=$(jq -r '.tool_input.command // ""')

sinks=$(printf '%s' "$cmd" | sed -E 's#>>?[[:space:]]*/(dev|tmp)/[^[:space:];&|)]*##g')

q="\\\\?['\"]"
mode="$q[rbt]*[wax+][rwaxbt+]*$q"

script_writes=(
  "open\(.*,[[:space:]]*(mode[[:space:]]*=[[:space:]]*)?$mode"
  "\.open\([[:space:]]*$mode"
  '\.write(lines)?\('
  '\.write_(text|bytes)\('
  '\.(unlink|touch)\('
  '(os|shutil)\.(rename|replace|remove|unlink|move|copy[a-z0-9]*)\('
  'fileinput'
  '(writeFile|appendFile|createWriteStream|renameSync|unlinkSync|rmSync|copyFile)'
  'fs\.(write|rename|unlink|rm)\('
  '(File|IO)\.write'
  "File\.open\(.*,[[:space:]]*$mode"
  'FileUtils'
)
script_writes=$(IFS='|'; printf '%s' "${script_writes[*]}")

script=$(printf '%s' "$cmd" | sed -E 's#(sys\.|process\.)?std(out|err)\.write##g')

reason=''

printf '%s' "$cmd" |
  grep -Eq '(^|[[:space:];&|(])(python3?|ruby|node)[[:space:]]+(-c([[:space:]]|$)|-[[:space:]]*(<<|$))' &&
  printf '%s' "$script" | grep -Eq "$script_writes" &&
  reason='an inline interpreter script that writes files'

printf '%s' "$cmd" |
  grep -Eq '(^|[[:space:];&|(])perl[[:space:]]+[^|;&]*-[[:alnum:]]*i' &&
  reason='a perl in-place edit'

printf '%s' "$cmd" |
  grep -Eq '(^|[[:space:];&|(])sed[[:space:]]+[^|;&]*-i([[:space:]]|$)' &&
  reason='a sed in-place edit'

printf '%s' "$sinks" |
  grep -Eq '(^|[[:space:];&|(])tee([[:space:]]|$)' &&
  reason='a tee write'

printf '%s' "$sinks" |
  grep -Eq '>>?[[:space:]]*"?[^[:space:];&|"]*(\.(c|h|R|r|md|json|yml|yaml|Rd)([[:space:]]|$|;)|(src|tests|plans|man|R)/)' &&
  reason='a shell redirect into a file'

if [ -n "$reason" ]; then
  jq -n --arg reason "$reason" '{
    hookSpecificOutput: {
      hookEventName: "PreToolUse",
      permissionDecision: "deny",
      permissionDecisionReason: (
        "Blocked: this is " + $reason + ". rray requires the Read, Edit and Write tools for all file access and edits. Use Edit instead, with replace_all for multi-site renames. Reading with cat, sed -n and grep is still fine, as is clang-format -i and air format."
      )
    }
  }'
fi
{
  "hooks": {
    "PreToolUse": [
      {
        "matcher": "Bash",
        "hooks": [
          {
            "type": "command",
            "command": ".claude/hooks/deny-shell-edits.sh",
            "statusMessage": "Checking for shell file edits..."
          }
        ]
      }
    ]
  }
}

make_array <- function(dimensions, type) {
  n <- prod(dimensions)

  values <- switch(
    type,
    logical = sample(c(TRUE, FALSE), n, replace = TRUE),
    integer = sample(100L, n, replace = TRUE),
    double = runif(n),
    complex = complex(real = runif(n), imaginary = runif(n))
  )

  array(values, dimensions)
}

set.seed(1)

results <- list()

for (operation in names(operations)) {
  fn <- operations[[operation]]$fn

  for (type in operations[[operation]]$types) {
    for (shape in names(shapes)) {
      x <- make_array(shapes[[shape]][[1L]], type)
      axes <- shapes[[shape]][[2L]]

      measurement <- bench::mark(
        fn(x, axes),
        iterations = iterations,
        check = FALSE,
        memory = FALSE,
        filter_gc = FALSE
      )

      results[[length(results) + 1L]] <- data.frame(
        operation = operation,
        type = type,
        shape = shape,
        median_ms = as.numeric(measurement$median) * 1000
      )
    }
  }
}

results <- do.call(rbind, results)

print(results, row.names = FALSE)

if (nzchar(output)) {
  saveRDS(
    list(
      results = results,
      iterations = iterations,
      revision = system2("git", c("rev-parse", "HEAD"), stdout = TRUE)
    ),
    output
  )
}

    36	  expect_identical(rray_dimensions(out), c(1L, 1L, 4L))

    40	  x <- array(1:24, c(2L, 3L, 4L))

    46	test_that("coalesces reduction axes", {
    47	  x <- array(1:24, c(1L, 3L, 2L, 4L))
    48	  expected <- function(axes) {
    49	    dimensions <- dim(x)
    50	    dimensions[axes] <- 1L
    51	    kept_axes <- setdiff(seq_along(dimensions), axes)
    52	
    53	    values <- if (length(kept_axes)) {
    54	      apply(x, kept_axes, sum)
    55	    } else {
    56	      sum(x)
    57	    }
    58	
    59	    array(values, dimensions)
    60	  }
    61	
    62	  axes <- list(1L, 2L, 3L, 4L, c(1L, 3L), c(2L, 4L), 1:4)
    63	
    64	  for (axis in axes) {
    65	    expect_identical(rray_sum(x, axis), expected(axis))
    66	    expect_identical(rray_sum_forced_fallback(x, axis), expected(axis))
    67	  }
    68	})

Write /private/tmp/claude-502/-Users-davis-files-r-packages-rray/f70e32fc-ff6d-4b22-90df-72e2d19203… 
args <- commandArgs(trailingOnly = TRUE)
fns <- strsplit(args[[1L]], ",")[[1L]]
files <- args[-1L]

walk <- function(expr, file) {
  if (!is.call(expr)) {
    return(invisible())
  }

  head <- expr[[1L]]
  if (is.symbol(head) && as.character(head) %in% fns) {
    call_args <- as.list(expr)[-1L]
    arg_names <- names(call_args) %||% rep("", length(call_args))
    if (length(call_args) >= 2L && arg_names[[2L]] == "") {
      cat(file, ": ", paste(deparse(expr), collapse = " "), "\n", sep = "")
    }
  }

  for (i in seq_along(expr)) {
    if (!missing(i) && !is.null(expr[[i]])) {
      walk(expr[[i]], file)
    }
  }
}

`%||%` <- function(x, y) if (is.null(x)) y else x

for (file in files) {
  exprs <- tryCatch(parse(file, keep.source = FALSE), error = function(e) NULL)
  if (is.null(exprs)) {
    cat("PARSE FAILED: ", file, "\n", sep = "")
    next
  }
  for (expr in exprs) {
    walk(expr, file)
  }
}

test_that("last position matches the reducer", {
  x <- array(c(2L, -1L, 5L, 4L, -3L, 7L), c(2L, 3L))
  expect_identical(
    rray_cumulative_sum(x, 2L)[, 3L, drop = FALSE],
    rray_sum(x, 2L)
  )

  x <- array(c(2, -1, 0.5, 4, -3, 0.25), c(3L, 2L))
  expect_identical(
    rray_cumulative_sum(x, 1L)[3L, , drop = FALSE],
    rray_sum(x, 1L)
  )

  x <- array(c(1 + 1i, 2i, -1 + 0i, 3 - 1i), c(2L, 2L))
  expect_identical(
    rray_cumulative_sum(x, 1L)[2L, , drop = FALSE],
    rray_sum(x, 1L)
  )
})

skip_if_not_testing_long_vectors <- function() {
  skip_if_not(
    identical(Sys.getenv("RRAY_TESTING_LONG_VECTORS"), "true"),
    "Not testing long vectors"
  )
}

skip_if_long_double <- function() {
  skip_if(
    !is.null(.Machine$longdouble.digits),
    "Base R accumulates in `long double`"
  )
}

set -eu

test_that("dimension names are dropped when all named axes are reduced", {
  x <- array(1:10, c(5L, 2L), dimnames = list(letters[1:5], NULL))
  expect_null(dimnames(rray_max(x, 1L)))
  expect_null(dimnames(rray_min(x, 1L)))
})

test_that("output type is the input type", {
  expect_identical(rray_max(c(TRUE, FALSE), 1L), array(TRUE, 1L))
  expect_identical(rray_min(c(TRUE, FALSE), 1L), array(FALSE, 1L))
  expect_identical(rray_max(c(1L, 2L), 1L), array(2L, 1L))
  expect_identical(rray_min(c(1L, 2L), 1L), array(1L, 1L))
  expect_identical(rray_max(c(1, 2), 1L), array(2, 1L))
  expect_identical(rray_min(c(1, 2), 1L), array(1, 1L))
})

test_that("matches repeated `pmax()` and `pmin()` across special values", {
  cases <- list(
    list(
      values = c(NA, FALSE, TRUE),
    }
  }
})

test_that("missing values propagate", {
  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_max(c(TRUE, NA), 1L)), NA)
  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_min(c(NA, FALSE), 1L)), NA)
  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_max(c(1L, NA), 1L)), NA_integer_)
  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_min(c(NA, 1L), 1L)), NA_integer_)
  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_max(c(1, NaN), 1L)), NaN)
  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_min(c(NA, 1), 1L)), NA_real_)
})

test_that("`NA` wins over `NaN`, like `max()` and `min()`", {
  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_max(c(NA, NaN), 1L)), NA_real_)
  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_max(c(NaN, NA), 1L)), NA_real_)
  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_max(c(NaN, 1, NA, NaN), 1L)), NA_real_)
  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_min(c(NA, NaN), 1L)), NA_real_)
  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_min(c(NaN, NA), 1L)), NA_real_)
  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_min(c(NaN, 1, NA, NaN), 1L)), NA_real_)
})

test_that("na_rm removes missing values", {
  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_max(c(NA, FALSE), 1L, na_rm = TRUE)), FALSE)
  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_min(c(TRUE, NA), 1L, na_rm = TRUE)), TRUE)
  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_max(c(NA, -1L), 1L, na_rm = TRUE)), -1L)
  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_min(c(1L, NA), 1L, na_rm = TRUE)), 1L)
  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_max(c(NaN, -1, NA), 1L, na_rm = TRUE)), -1)
  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_min(c(NA, 1, NaN), 1L, na_rm = TRUE)), 1)
})

test_that("na_rm with all missing returns the identity value", {
  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_max(c(NA, NA), 1L, na_rm = TRUE)), FALSE)
  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_min(c(NA, NA), 1L, na_rm = TRUE)), TRUE)

  x <- c(NA_integer_, NA_integer_)
  expect_identical(
    as.vector(rray_max(x, 1L, na_rm = TRUE)),
    -.Machine$integer.max
  )
  expect_identical(
    as.vector(rray_min(x, 1L, na_rm = TRUE)),
    .Machine$integer.max
  )

  x <- c(NA, NaN)
  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_max(x, 1L, na_rm = TRUE)), -Inf)
  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_min(x, 1L, na_rm = TRUE)), Inf)
})

test_that("na_rm with no missing values matches default", {
  x <- array(c(3L, 1L, 2L, 4L, 6L, 5L), c(3L, 2L))
  expect_identical(rray_max(x, 1L, na_rm = TRUE), rray_max(x, 1L))
  expect_identical(rray_min(x, 1L, na_rm = TRUE), rray_min(x, 1L))
})

test_that("the most extreme values of each type are reachable", {
  x <- c(-.Machine$integer.max, -.Machine$integer.max)
  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_max(x, 1L)), -.Machine$integer.max)

  x <- c(.Machine$integer.max, .Machine$integer.max)
  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_min(x, 1L)), .Machine$integer.max)

  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_max(c(-Inf, -Inf), 1L)), -Inf)
  expect_identical(as.vector(rray_min(c(Inf, Inf), 1L)), Inf)
})

test_that("plain vector input works", {
  expect_identical(rray_max(c(1L, 3L, 2L), 1L), array(3L, 1L))
  expect_identical(rray_min(c(1L, 3L, 2L), 1L), array(1L, 1L))
})

test_that("axes are coerced to integer", {
  x <- array(1:4, c(2L, 2L))
  expect_identical(rray_max(x, 1), rray_max(x, 1L))
  expect_identical(rray_min(x, 1), rray_min(x, 1L))
})

test_that("reducing a zero-length axis gives the identity value", {
  zero_size <- function(x) array(x, c(0L, 2L))

  expect_identical(
    rray_max(zero_size(logical()), 1L),
    array(FALSE, c(1L, 2L))
  )
  expect_identical(
    rray_min(zero_size(logical()), 1L),
    array(TRUE, c(1L, 2L))
  )
  expect_identical(
    rray_max(zero_size(integer()), 1L),
    array(-.Machine$integer.max, c(1L, 2L))
  )
  expect_identical(
    rray_min(zero_size(integer()), 1L),
    array(.Machine$integer.max, c(1L, 2L))
  )
  expect_identical(
    rray_max(zero_size(double()), 1L),
    array(-Inf, c(1L, 2L))
  )

1	test_that("can locate along axis 1", {
2	  x <- array(c(3L, 1L, 2L, 4L, 6L, 5L), c(3L, 2L))
3	  expect_identical(rray_locate_max(x, 1L), array(c(1L, 2L), c(1L, 2L)))
4	  expect_identical(rray_locate_min(x, 1L), array(c(2L, 1L), c(1L, 2L)))
5	})
6	
7	test_that("can locate along axis 2", {
8	  x <- array(c(3L, 1L, 2L, 4L, 0L, 5L), c(3L, 2L))
9	  expect_identical(rray_locate_max(x, 2L), array(c(2L, 1L, 2L), c(3L, 1L)))
10	  expect_identical(rray_locate_min(x, 2L), array(c(1L, 2L, 1L), c(3L, 1L)))
11	})

13	test_that("can locate along axis 3", {
14	  x <- array(c(1:6, 12:7, 13:18), c(2L, 3L, 3L))
15	  expect_identical(rray_locate_max(x, 3L), array(3L, c(2L, 3L, 1L)))

17	})

19	test_that("matches the oracle along every axis", {
20	  x <- array((1:72 * 7L) %% 11L, c(2L, 3L, 1L, 4L, 3L))
21	  x[c(5L, 30L, 31L, 64L)] <- NA
22	
23	  for (axis in seq_along(dim(x))) {
24	    for (na_rm in c(FALSE, TRUE)) {
25	      expect_identical(
26	        rray_locate_max(x, axis, na_rm = na_rm),
27	        base_locate(x, axis, which.max, na_rm)
28	      )
29	      expect_identical(
30	        rray_locate_min(x, axis, na_rm = na_rm),
31	        base_locate(x, axis, which.min, na_rm)
32	      )
33	    }
34	  }
35	})
36	
37	test_that("matches the oracle and `rray_max()` across special values", {
38	  cases <- list(
39	    c(NA, FALSE, TRUE),
40	    c(NA, -.Machine$integer.max, 0L, .Machine$integer.max),
41	    c(NA, NaN, -Inf, -1, 0, 1, Inf)
42	  )
43	
44	  for (values in cases) {
45	    x <- unname(t(as.matrix(expand.grid(rep(list(values), 3L)))))
46	    lanes <- seq_len(ncol(x))
47	
48	    for (na_rm in c(FALSE, TRUE)) {
49	      max_loc <- rray_locate_max(x, 1L, na_rm = na_rm)
50	      min_loc <- rray_locate_min(x, 1L, na_rm = na_rm)
51	
52	      expect_identical(max_loc, base_locate(x, 1L, which.max, na_rm))
53	      expect_identical(min_loc, base_locate(x, 1L, which.min, na_rm))
54	
55	      expect_identical(
56	        rray_locate_max(t(x), 2L, na_rm = na_rm),
57	        t(max_loc)
58	      )
59	      expect_identical(
60	        rray_locate_min(t(x), 2L, na_rm = na_rm),
61	        t(min_loc)
62	      )
63	    }
64	
65	    max_loc <- as.vector(rray_locate_max(x, 1L))
66	    min_loc <- as.vector(rray_locate_min(x, 1L))
67	    max <- as.vector(rray_max(x, axes = 1L))
68	    min <- as.vector(rray_min(x, axes = 1L))
69	
70	    expect_identical(is.na(max_loc), is.na(max))
71	    expect_identical(is.na(min_loc), is.na(min))
72	
73	    max_found <- !is.na(max_loc)
74	    min_found <- !is.na(min_loc)
75	
76	    expect_identical(
77	      x[cbind(max_loc[max_found], lanes[max_found])],
78	      max[max_found]
79	    )
80	    expect_identical(
81	      x[cbind(min_loc[min_found], lanes[min_found])],
82	      min[min_found]
83	    )
84	  }
85	})
86	
87	test_that("ties return the first position", {
88	  expect_identical(rray_locate_max(c(1L, 3L, 3L), 1L), array(2L, 1L))
89	  expect_identical(rray_locate_min(c(3L, 1L, 1L), 1L), array(2L, 1L))
90	  expect_identical(rray_locate_max(c(FALSE, TRUE, TRUE), 1L), array(2L, 1L))
91	  expect_identical(rray_locate_min(c(TRUE, FALSE, FALSE), 1L), array(2L, 1L))
92	  expect_identical(rray_locate_max(c(-Inf, -Inf), 1L), array(1L, 1L))
93	  expect_identical(rray_locate_min(c(Inf, Inf), 1L), array(1L, 1L))
94	})
95	
96	test_that("signed zeros tie", {
97	  expect_identical(rray_locate_max(c(-0, 0), 1L), array(1L, 1L))
98	  expect_identical(rray_locate_max(c(0, -0), 1L), array(1L, 1L))
99	  expect_identical(rray_locate_min(c(-0, 0), 1L), array(1L, 1L))
100	  expect_identical(rray_locate_min(c(0, -0), 1L), array(1L, 1L))
101	  expect_identical(rray_locate_max(t(c(-0, 0)), 2L), array(1L, c(1L, 1L)))
102	  expect_identical(rray_locate_min(t(c(0, -0)), 2L), array(1L, c(1L, 1L)))
103	})
104	
105	test_that("missing values give `NA`", {
106	  na <- array(NA_integer_, 1L)
107	  expect_identical(rray_locate_max(c(TRUE, NA, FALSE), 1L), na)
108	  expect_identical(rray_locate_min(c(FALSE, NA, TRUE), 1L), na)
109	  expect_identical(rray_locate_max(c(NA, TRUE), 1L), na)
110	  expect_identical(rray_locate_min(c(NA, FALSE), 1L), na)
111	  expect_identical(rray_locate_max(c(1L, NA, 2L), 1L), na)
112	  expect_identical(rray_locate_min(c(1L, NA, 0L), 1L), na)
113	  expect_identical(rray_locate_max(c(NA, 1L), 1L), na)
114	  expect_identical(rray_locate_min(c(NA, 1L), 1L), na)
115	  expect_identical(rray_locate_max(c(1, NaN, 2), 1L), na)
116	  expect_identical(rray_locate_min(c(1, NA, 0), 1L), na)
117	  expect_identical(rray_locate_max(c(NaN, 1), 1L), na)
118	  expect_identical(rray_locate_min(c(NA, 1), 1L), na)
119	})

121	test_that("na_rm skips missing values", {
122	  x <- c(NA, FALSE, TRUE)
123	  expect_identical(rray_locate_max(x, 1L, na_rm = TRUE), array(3L, 1L))
124	  expect_identical(rray_locate_min(x, 1L, na_rm = TRUE), array(2L, 1L))
125	
126	  x <- c(NA, -.Machine$integer.max, NA)
127	  expect_identical(rray_locate_max(x, 1L, na_rm = TRUE), array(2L, 1L))
128	  expect_identical(rray_locate_min(x, 1L, na_rm = TRUE), array(2L, 1L))
129	
130	  x <- c(NaN, -Inf, NA, Inf)
131	  expect_identical(rray_locate_max(x, 1L, na_rm = TRUE), array(4L, 1L))
132	  expect_identical(rray_locate_min(x, 1L, na_rm = TRUE), array(2L, 1L))
133	})

185	test_that("dimension names are kept for the other axes", {
186	  x <- array(1:10, c(5L, 2L), dimnames = list(letters[1:5], c("c1", "c2")))
187	  expect_identical(dimnames(rray_locate_max(x, 1L)), list(NULL, c("c1", "c2")))
188	  expect_identical(dimnames(rray_locate_min(x, 2L)), list(letters[1:5], NULL))
189	})

194	  expect_null(dimnames(rray_locate_min(x, 1L)))

   128	  expect_identical(dimnames(rray_cumulative_all(x, 1L)), dimnames(x))
   129	  expect_identical(dimnames(rray_cumulative_any(x, 2L)), dimnames(x))
   130	
   131	  x <- array(TRUE, c(2L, 2L), dimnames = list(NULL, c("c", "d")))
   132	  expect_identical(dimnames(rray_cumulative_all(x, 2L)), dimnames(x))
   133	  expect_identical(dimnames(rray_cumulative_any(x, 2L)), dimnames(x))
   134	
   135	  x <- array(TRUE, c(2L, 2L))
   136	  expect_null(dimnames(rray_cumulative_all(x, 1L)))
   137	  expect_null(dimnames(rray_cumulative_any(x, 1L)))
   138	})
   139	
   140	test_that("titles are kept", {
   141	  x <- array(
   142	    TRUE,
   143	    c(2L, 2L),
   144	    dimnames = list(Row = c("a", "b"), Column = NULL)
   145	  )
   146	  expect_identical(rray_titles(rray_cumulative_all(x, 1L)), c("Row", "Column"))
   147	  expect_identical(rray_titles(rray_cumulative_any(x, 2L)), c("Row", "Column"))
   148	})
   149	
   150	test_that("vectors become 1D arrays", {
   151	  expect_identical(
   152	    rray_cumulative_all(c(a = TRUE, b = NA, c = FALSE), 1L),
   153	    array(c(TRUE, NA, FALSE), 3L, dimnames = list(c("a", "b", "c")))
   154	  )
   155	  expect_identical(
   156	    rray_cumulative_any(c(FALSE, NA, TRUE), 1L),

   158	  )
   159	})
   160

   164	  expect_identical(rray_cumulative_any(x, 2), rray_cumulative_any(x, 2L))
   165	})
   166	
   167	test_that("`axis` is validated", {

   169	
   170	  expect_snapshot(rray_cumulative_all(x, integer()), error = TRUE)
   171	  expect_snapshot(rray_cumulative_all(x, 1:2), error = TRUE)
   172	  expect_snapshot(rray_cumulative_all(x, NA_integer_), error = TRUE)
   173	  expect_snapshot(rray_cumulative_all(x, 0L), error = TRUE)
   174	  expect_snapshot(rray_cumulative_all(x, -1L), error = TRUE)

   178	  expect_snapshot(rray_cumulative_any(x, "a"), error = TRUE)
   179	})

   184	})

test_that("`axis` is validated", {
  x <- array(1:6, c(2, 3))
  expect_snapshot(rray_unstack(x, axis = 0), error = TRUE)
  expect_snapshot(rray_unstack(x, axis = 3), error = TRUE)
  expect_snapshot(rray_unstack(x, axis = c(1, 2)), error = TRUE)
  expect_snapshot(rray_unstack(x, axis = NA_integer_), error = TRUE)
  expect_snapshot(rray_unstack(x, axis = 1.5), error = TRUE)
  expect_snapshot(rray_unstack(x, axis = structure(1L, class = "foo")), error = TRUE)
})

test_that("1D arrays are rejected", {
  expect_snapshot(rray_unstack(array(1:3), axis = 1), error = TRUE)
  expect_snapshot(rray_unstack(1:3, axis = 1), error = TRUE)
})

test_that("errors on invalid input", {
  expect_snapshot(rray_unstack(NULL, axis = 1), error = TRUE)

  x <- structure(array(1:4, c(2, 2)), class = "foo")
  expect_snapshot(rray_unstack(x, axis = 1), error = TRUE)
})

I've implemented plans/dots.md as 17 commits, one per function in checklist order, and pushed them to PR #149 (feature/dots). Every commit passed devtools::test() and devtools::check() on its own with 0 errors, 0 warnings and 0 notes.

What each commit does

  • Moves axis or axes behind .... The cumulative functions, rray_reverse() and rray_unstack() also gained check_dots_empty0(...) and @inheritParams rlang::args_dots_empty.

  • Names the axis at every call site: roxygen examples, the code in docs prose (such as the rray_sum() example in R/cumulative.R), tests, and the two _forced_fallback() test helpers.

  • Adds a "axis must be named" (or "axes must be named") test next to the other argument tests. Where two functions share a test file (max/min, all/any, cumulative max/min), the second function adds a line to the first one's test instead of a duplicate block.

  • Ticks the function off in the plan's checklist.

README.Rmd and the vignettes already named every axis, so they didn't change.

Things to check

  • Long-vector snapshots. snapshot_accept() deleted the snapshots for the three skipped long-vector rray_sum() tests. I put them back by hand in _snaps/reduce-sum.md, changing only the call text to axes = 1:2. I didn't run those tests (they need about 8 to 16 GB). Run them with RRAY_TESTING_LONG_VECTORS=true if you want to confirm.

  • bench/. The plan doesn't mention it, but bench/reduce.R, bench/mean.R and bench/cumulative.R would have broken, so I named the axis there in the matching commits.

  • bench/stride-zero.R is left alone. It calls rray_max(matrix_x, scalar) and rray_min(matrix_x, scalar), meaning the element-wise max that is now rray_pmax(). That was already broken before this PR.

  • The new snapshot for rray_unstack() uses a 2D array, rray_unstack(array(1:4, c(2, 2)), 1), rather than the plan's 1:3. The dots error would come first either way, but a matrix is a valid input to unstack, so the test reads more naturally.

  • Branch. The session setup said to push to feature/titles, but you said "this PR" and the plan names feature/dots, so I pushed there.
